#### 8.6.7 Substrate permutation model and the tick normalization

【Derivation + numerics】The claim in §4.2 that "pair annihilation–regeneration under 50:50 is dynamically equivalent to elastic scattering" requires a concrete mechanism; this subsection supplies it and fixes its **normalization**.

**(a) Mechanism**: treat one tick as applying a single **nearest-neighbour transposition** to the occupancy (an adjacent generator of the symmetric group S_N). A transposition is a bijection ⟹ the number of elements is automatically conserved, every site carries exactly one element, and the vacuum state never occurs (it is a logical state, not an occupancy); adjacent transpositions generate all of S_N and are invertible ⟹ **no entropy is produced at the fundamental level, so macroscopic irreversibility must come from coarse-graining**. Nearest-neighbour transposition = identical particles exchanging identity = 1D equal-mass elastic collision (velocity exchange), hence indistinguishable from elastic scattering at the many-body level.

**(b) Tick normalization (new in v2.9; important)**: what actually needs adjudication is **how many transpositions occur per tick**, not whether they are synchronous. A1's `ν ~ 1/t_P` is the event rate **per element** (not for the whole system), so

$$\text{exactly one event per site per tick}\qquad(\text{parallel normalization})$$

is the only choice on the same footing as A1. Taking "one global transposition per tick" (serial normalization) conflicts with three independent criteria:

| Criterion | Parallel | Serial | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 per-element event rate `1/t_P` | `1/t_P` ✓ | `2/(N_c t_P)`, off by `10^182.5` ✗ | serial excluded |
| A2 leakage fraction `ε₀ = 7.7×10⁻²⁰` (per event) | directly usable ✓ | must be amplified by `3.06×10¹⁸²` ⟹ ε₀ ≫ 1 ✗ | serial excluded |
| §4.2 velocity-space relaxation ~4 transpositions | exactly 4 within 4 t_P ✓ | probability `1.3×10⁻¹⁸²` within 4 t_P ✗ | serial excluded |

(`N_c = R_c/l_P = 8.4918×10⁶⁰`, `N_c³ = 6.1236×10¹⁸²`.)

**(c) Implementation**: under parallel normalization, "every site participates exactly once per tick" requires pairing sites into a **bipartite perfect matching** (the 3D cubic lattice is bipartite; odd–even pairing along the chosen axis suffices). Isotropy and true diffusion require randomizing two things:

- **random axis** (x/y/z chosen at random each tick) — a fixed axis gives one-dimensional diffusion with the other two axes frozen;
- **random phase** (**independently per line**, redrawn each tick) — two shortcuts both fail: (i) with a fixed phase, a particle's parity role on that axis is locked: one step flips its parity, so the next step sends it back, producing a two-cycle oscillation with bounded displacement (numerically ⟨r²⟩ = 2 against a diffusive value of 600); (ii) **even if the phase is redrawn every tick, a single global phase** (all lines on the same side within one tick) couples the phase to the axis into a systematic orientation pattern, ⟨r²⟩ deviating from n and being **not self-averaging** (retested in round 43 with 20 seeds: mean **1.32 n**, relative scatter **65%**, range [0.364, 3.444], versus **1.001 ± 0.027**, scatter **3%**, for independent per-line phases). **Per-line independence is therefore a requirement, not an implementation preference.** (The original figures "29.7% / 42.5% (two seeds)" were noise from a non-self-averaging quantity, and the direction of "collapsing" was also wrong: in this mode ⟨r²⟩ has a mean above n with a huge scatter. Corrected in round 43.)

Numerical verification (L = 10, N = 1000 sites, 2000 ticks): ⟨r²⟩ = 1981.3 (= n, deviation 0.9%), ⟨x²⟩:⟨y²⟩:⟨z²⟩ = 672.9 : 694.2 : 614.2 (each ≈ n/3, within 3σ), zero vacuum events, permutation preserved every tick, no net drift.

**(d) Which normalization the 47 μm belongs to**: the slow timescale `R_mix = l_P√(t/t_P) = 47 μm` per Hubble time in §4.2 **is the parallel-normalization value** (one l_P step per element per t_P). Under the serial normalization the same expression becomes `l_P√(2t/(N_c³ t_P)) = 2.7×10⁻⁹⁶ m`, sixty-one orders below the Planck length — physically unacceptable. The 47 μm figure and the ruling in (b) therefore support each other.

**(e) Price**: parallel normalization defines a **globally simultaneous tick**, i.e. a preferred frame for the substrate. This makes C-2 (Planck-scale absoluteness must not leak into 1PN; the PPN parameters ξ, α₁, α₂, α₃ all vanish in §4.5b) a **necessary condition**: A1's 50:50 and isotropy must be what erases the substrate's absoluteness. Parallel updating promotes C-2 from optional to required.

> ⚠️ **【Round-93 note (N-38): the "must be responsible" has not been demonstrated】** "A1's 50:50 + isotropy" is a symmetry of the **fluctuation statistics**, whereas the α/ξ parameters describe **sensitivity to motion relative to a preferred frame** — **isotropy ≠ boost invariance** (a fully isotropic medium still defines a preferred frame: acoustic media are the counterexample; the tick's constant-time-surface normal `n^μ` is structurally the same kind of object as the aether `u^μ`, and an isotropic aether can still give `α₁ ≠ 0`). Project-wide search: **no calculation of any "leakage" exists** (the words "demonstrated / proved" never co-occur with "absoluteness"; the registrations are only **assignments**). ⟹ **C-2 is upgraded to an undemonstrated necessary condition**; **I-22** registered. ⚠️ C-2 is also **one of the preconditions for a clean EMRI verdict**.
> 📌 **Disambiguation (C-50)**: "C-2" here = the **round-18 commitment series** (C-1 occupancy f=1 / C-2 no leakage / C-3 mass = reversal probability); **not** the checklist's correction "C-2" (round-50 H/N convention), and **not** the round-74 backlog group's `C5 = PPN preferred frame`. **Any citation of "C-n" must state its series.**

**【v2.9 supplement (round 42: the exogenous tick randomness is the source of entropy growth)】** This subsection takes the tick axis/phase to be random; this round computes the **quantitative consequence**:

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| Choices per tick | 3 axes × 2^(L²) (L = lattice side, #lines = L²) |
| Ignorance injected per tick | `h = ln3 + L² ln2` nats/tick |
| Macroscopic (L ≈ N_c = 8.4918×10⁶⁰) | `h = 4.998×10¹²¹` nats/tick, `ḣ = h/t_P = 9.27×10¹⁶⁴` nats/s |
| Horizon BH entropy | `S_BH/k_B = πN_c² = 2.265×10¹²²` nats |
| **Time to reach S_BH** | **`S_BH/h = π/ln2 = 4.532 ticks ≈ 2.44×10⁻⁴³ s` (analytic)** |

⟹ **Statistical mixing is instantaneous**: the ensemble entropy reaches the horizon's BH entropy in about 4.5 Planck times. This differs from the **spatial transport** of §4.2 ② (`R_mix(t_H) = 47 μm` per Hubble time) by about **10⁶⁰** — they are **two different things**: the mixing of *information* versus the transport of *matter*, and they do not conflict. ⟹ This also supplies the **dynamical reason** for round 40's "equipartition holds instantaneously / needs no thermalisation": the exogenous tick randomness drives the ensemble to equipartition within ~4.5 ticks.

⚠️ **Upper bound on entropy growth**: the ensemble entropy saturates at `ln|O|` (the orbit size), and `ln|O| ≤ ln C(N, N/2) ≈ 4.24×10¹⁸²` nats ≫ `S_BH` (about 1.9×10⁶⁰ times) ⟹ **capacity is not the bottleneck; the rate is**. After saturation there is no further growth.

⚠️ **First adjudication of T-7 (negative)**: is the tick randomness "exogenous true randomness" (H1) or "an underlying determinism, merely coarse-grained" (H2)? This round shows that H2 must encode the tick choice in the substrate itself, at a cost of (#bits of the tick choice ≈ L²) / (#sites L³) = **1/L → 0** (= 1.18×10⁻⁶¹ bits per site macroscopically) ⟹ it does **not** conflict with the sub-binary density "0.25 nat per l_P²" ⟹ **H1 and H2 are empirically equivalent at the level of the visible substrate and cannot be distinguished**. ⟹ Whether the future is predictable *in principle* is **undecidable within this framework**; under the **current formulation (H1)** the answer is definite: **not predictable** (the future is not a function of the present state).

⚠️ **Coexists with round 21**: a single tick is still a bijection ⟹ the information of the *state* is strictly conserved; what this round computes is the growth of the *ensemble* (ignorance) entropy. The two do not conflict — "no information is lost" is about the state, "entropy grows" is about our ignorance of it.

> **【v2.9 completion (round 81: quantifying the "correlation length" — it is not a new quantity, it is already written into `h`)】** An old list item read "**quantify the correlation length**" (round 22's observation: with a fixed axis the spread across seeds far exceeds the independent-sample estimate ⟹ **effective d.o.f. < number of sites**; marked "**not done**" at the time). This round completes it (script `_h1h4_81.py`).
>
> **Caliber**: one tick along the x axis, **one independent phase per x-line** (exactly the framework's A1). Observable `u_i = ±1` (site i is exchanged towards +x or −x).
>
> | Quantity | Result |
> |---|---|
> | **Along-axis** correlation `C∥(d)` | `= (−1)^d` — **bit-exact** (**full enumeration**, zero noise): **period 2, never decaying** |
> | **Transverse** correlation `C⊥` | single-realization deviation is **exactly `1/L`** (0.2500 / 0.1667 / 0.1250 at L = 4/6/8); after averaging over **300 phase realizations `max|C⊥| = 0.0204`** ⟹ **different lines are independent (0)** |
> | **Effective d.o.f.** | `N_eff = **L²**` (**number of lines**), not `L³` (number of sites) — MC: 16.09 / 36.46 / 65.83 / 144.77 vs `L²` = 16 / 36 / 64 / 144 ✓ |
>
> ⟹ ⭐ **The "correlation length" has no finite value; it is dichotomous**: along the axis `ξ∥ = ∞` (with a `(−1)^d` modulation), perpendicular to it `ξ⊥ = 0`.
> **Mechanism**: the tick's **randomness unit is the "line"** (a 1D object, one phase bit per line), **not the site**.
> ⟹ ⭐ **It is not a new quantity — it is already written into `h = ln3 + L²ln2`**: that **`L²` is the number of lines**. What this round supplies is only its **spatial manifestation** (an anisotropic, dichotomous correlation function). This also gives the **mechanism** behind round 22's "`N_eff` ~ number of lines" (not a statistical accident but a structural consequence of "the randomness unit is a line").
> ⚠️ **Honest close-out (N-36)**: do **not** read the `L²` in `h` (an area scaling) as "the framework derives the area law" — it is `h`'s **defining expression**; and "lattice discretization ↔ area law" was formally **registered as NOT COVERED** in round 80, with only a loose inequality between B5 and the substrate (**N-13**) ⟹ **this entry provides no new support** (same type as round 77's "mistaking a definition for a discovery").

**【v2.9 supplement (round 43: h is not observable — T-7 falls to "the implementation is also undecidable", and the source of entropy growth is restated)**】** Round 42 proposed that "the two implementations of §8.6.7 give different diffusion coefficients ⟹ use D to infer h". This round computes that "only observable exit" to the end.

**Four phase modes** (simple cubic L³, periodic boundaries, one tick = perfect matching along a random axis; one phase φ per line):

| Phase mode | ⟨r²⟩/n (full lattice, L=10, n=2000) | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| independent per line (main implementation) | **1.0310** | exactly one step per tick ⟹ diffusion |
| globally fixed (constant phase) | **0.0010** (⟨r²⟩ = 2.00) | **strictly bounded** (matches round 26 (i)) |
| fixed per line | 0.3406 | suppressed but non-zero (not listed in round 26) |
| globally shared (redrawn each tick) | 0.6130 (single seed) | **not self-averaging**, see below |
| **deterministic LCG** (proxy for H2) | **1.0075** | **same value** as true randomness |

⚠️ **Methodological point**: **a single-marker trajectory cannot see the difference between "independent per line" and "globally shared"** — the marker only ever meets the φ of its own line (measured: 0.9933 for both, bit-for-bit). Phase sharing shows up **only in multi-element correlations** ⟹ judging phase modes from the transport of a single particle is invalid.

⚠️ **20-seed statistics (3D, L=10, n=2000)**: independent per line gives mean **1.001 ± 0.027** (relative scatter **3%**, self-averaging); globally shared gives mean **1.317**, range [0.364, 3.444] (relative scatter **65%**, **not self-averaging**). ⟹ **"30–40%" is not a structural value but noise from a non-self-averaging quantity** (the (c) paragraph above is corrected accordingly).

**Rulings (four)**:

1. **`h` is not observable**: the observable is D (**one real number**); the microscopic degrees of freedom are the per-line phases = **L² bits per tick** (macroscopically ≈ 7.2×10¹²¹) ⟹ **L² bits → 1 real number** — underdetermined, not merely "hard to measure".
2. **T-7's discriminating exit is closed**: true-random phases (D = 0.9700) versus deterministic LCG phases (D = 1.0704) differ by **0.1005 = 1.51σ** (within noise), while their h differ by **120 orders of magnitude** (4.998×10¹²¹ versus 44.36 nats once and for all) ⟹ "cross-check h with the diffusion coefficient" **fails**.
3. **Boundary of D's discriminating power**: "globally fixed" (bounded) and "globally shared" (non-self-averaging) **are** distinguishable ⟹ round 26's exclusion of the two shortcuts is an **observable** exclusion; but **"where the randomness comes from" is completely invisible at the transport level**. The mobility p is distinguishable (D ∝ p: p = 1/2 ⟹ 0.4727, p = 1/4 ⟹ 0.2487), and A1 already pins p = 1.
4. **Source of entropy growth restated**: round 42's "source of entropy growth = **exogenous tick randomness**" becomes "source of entropy growth = **ignorance** (ensemble entropy)"; and "where the ignorance comes from" (**a coin flipped in the vacuum**, H1, or **our not knowing the seed**, H2) is **undecidable within the framework**. This **merges** with round 39's "entropy growth = a measure of coarse-graining / indistinguishability": injection and coarse-graining are two narratives of one thing at the observable level.

⚠️ **Final T-7 position**: H1 and H2 agree on **all transport predictions** of the framework ⟹ T-7 falls from "ontology undecidable, implementation decidable" (round 42) to "**ontology undecidable, implementation also undecidable**". The only remaining entrance is the **two-point correlation function** (Table 2 shows the difference lives in multi-body correlations only); if that too agrees, T-7 can be closed.

**【v2.9 supplement (round 44: two-point correlations are also undecidable — T-7 is closed)**】** After round 43 ruled out single-body transport and noted that "any difference must hide in many-body correlations", this round computes those correlations: they are **equally undecidable**.

⚠️ **Methodological premise**: **"true randomness" in a numerical simulation is itself pseudo-random** (unless one uses `os.urandom`). This round therefore compares three tiers: `sys` = a true random source (the strict version of H1); `indep` = MT19937 (**deterministic but high-quality**, a good proxy for H2); `lcg` = a linear congruential generator (**deterministic and low-quality**, a poor proxy for H2).

**Spatial correlations** (`C = ⟨Δ_i·Δ_j⟩/⟨r²⟩`, full lattice L = 6, n = 1200, 5 seeds):

| mode | C_near(d=1) | C_far(d≥3) |
|---|---|---|
| true random source (H1) | **−0.03943** | −0.00460 |
| MT19937 (good H2 proxy) | **−0.03368** | −0.00245 |
| LCG (poor H2 proxy) | **−0.06014** | +0.00128 |

⟹ true source versus high-quality determinism differs by **0.14σ**; versus low-quality determinism by **0.42σ** — **both within noise**. Crucially, the correlation is **not zero** (≈ −0.04): the exclusion inherent in a transposition does produce displacement correlations ⟹ "undecidable" is **not** due to a weak observable.

**Temporal correlations** (`C(τ) = ⟨Δ(t)Δ(t+τ)⟩/⟨Δ²⟩`, L = 4, n = 4000):

| mode | C(τ=1) |
|---|---|
| true random source | −0.0090 |
| MT19937 (high-quality) | **−0.0009** (agrees with the true source) |
| LCG (low-quality) | **−0.3339** (≈ 37× the true source) |

⟹ Temporal correlations distinguish **low-quality** generators but **not high-quality** ones ⟹ they test the **quality of the pseudo-random generator**, **not** whether the randomness is exogenous.

⚠️ **The periodicity test fails** (a negative finding of this round): driving **both** the phases and the axis by a finite-state generator (period 256 ticks) gives an autocorrelation of **−0.3269** at τ = 256, whereas strict periodicity requires **C(T) ≈ +1**. ⟹ **The displacement sequence does not repeat with the driving period**: displacement is a difference of positions, and positions keep evolving without ever returning to the start ⟹ the period of the driving sequence is **not transferred verbatim** to the displacement. (Caveat: |C| = 0.327 at τ = 256 still far exceeds the true-source level ⟹ correlation structure does leave a trace at long lags ⟹ low-quality H2 remains distinguishable.)

**Final T-7 position**: the ruled-out entrances are ① entropy density (round 42), ② the diffusion coefficient D (round 43), ③ **spatial** two-point correlations (this round, 0.14σ), and ④ **periodicity** (this round, masked by state evolution); the only successful test, ⑤ **temporal** correlations, works only for low-quality generators. ⟹ Under the four-way verdict of round 37, **T-7 is "undecidable within the framework"**: it degenerates into a question about the quality of a pseudo-random generator, **not a physical question** — the framework does not, and cannot, stipulate which tier of generator the tick uses. **Under the current formulation (H1) the answer is: unpredictable** (the future is not a function of the present state).

> **【v2.9 supplement (round 65: the tick's "image" versus true white noise — the nine-criterion divide)】** Motivation: the simulator's canvas "looks exactly like TV snow". This round turns that observation into a **decidable** question: **on which statistics do tick-evolved substrate configurations and i.i.d. white noise separate?** (script `_snow_65.py`, SN-1…SN-9, **11 pass / 0 fail**)
>
> **The nine criteria** (lattice 12³ = 1728 sites, density 1/2; the tick configurations are the **actual end states after 30 ticks**, not random draws):
>
> | Tier | Criterion | tick | white noise | Verdict |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | **single frame / local** | single-site density | 0.500 | 0.499 | ❌ identical |
> | | nearest-neighbour two-point correlation C(1) | +1.0×10⁻⁴ | +5.1×10⁻⁴ | ❌ signal < noise floor 6.2×10⁻⁴ |
> | | block-density variance (coarse-graining) | 3.642×10⁻³ | 3.990×10⁻³ | ❌ 8.7% apart |
> | | **frame-to-frame bit-flip fraction Δ** | 0.503 | 0.500 | ❌ **identical** (see below) |
> | **time / conserved quantities** | **axis-aligned line-level rearrangement** | **1.0000** | **0.1802** | ✅ structural identity vs coincidence |
> | | **k (number of lit sites)** | range **0** | range **104** | ✅ conserved vs not |
> | | reversibility | bijective + involutive | irreversible | ✅ separable |
> | | **mixing time from an ordered initial state** | **62 steps** | **1 step** | ✅ 62× apart |
> | | **size scaling of the mixing time** | **τ ∝ L²** | τ = 1 | ✅ separable |
>
> **The divide is clean**: the first four live **entirely in "single frame / local" and are all inseparable**; the last five live **entirely in "time / conserved quantities" and are all separable**.
> ⟹ **Rule of thumb**: to discriminate "deterministic vs random", **do not compare spatial snapshots — compare time series and conserved quantities**: a snapshot is a single sample from the ensemble, and **ensemble distributions can be identical in both cases**; whatever separates them must live in the **path**.
> ⚠️ The hardest of the nine is "axis-aligned line-level rearrangement": for tick it is a **structural identity** (the number of lit sites on **every line** along the swapped axis must be preserved, because the swap happens strictly inside one line), whereas for white noise it is only a **probabilistic coincidence** (≈ `C(24,12)/2²⁴` = 0.1612).
>
> **The τ ∝ L² data points** (ordered initial state ⟹ |left-half density − 0.5| first drops below 2× the hypergeometric floor): L = 6 / 8 / 10 give τ = 15 / 23 / 46, so **τ/L² = 0.42 / 0.36 / 0.46 ⟹ stable** (L = 4 is too small and was excluded).
> **Mechanism**: tick swaps **adjacent pairs** ⟹ it is a **local diffusive** step, not a global redraw (a global redraw would give a τ independent of L).
> ⚠️ This is the **same scaling** as §4.2's `R_mix = l_P√(t/t_P)` (`R² ∝ t` ⟺ `t ∝ L²`), but from a **different source** (relaxation of a permutation chain versus a diffusion equation) ⟹ **no claim of deep unification** (same reservation as round 59).
>
> ⚠️ **A negative finding of this round (my own prediction refuted)**: the reasoning was "tick moves only one axis ⟹ 1/3 chance; and since the swap is done in pairs, half the pairs 'swap to no effect' ⟹ Δ_tick = 1/4, half as large as white noise". **Measured 0.503 ≈ 0.500 — identical.**
> Why: **every site is necessarily rewritten** as "its partner's old value" (the phase only decides **who the partner is**, and **the partner is never the site itself**) ⟹ `P(value changes) = P(partner differs) = 1/2`.
> ⟹ **the bit-flip fraction is not a criterion** (registered as **N-24**).
>
> ⚠️ **Three honest boundaries**: ① "true randomness" in a browser or a script can only be **pseudo-random** (the hard limit of round 44) ⟹ strictly, this round compares a "high-quality deterministic **permutation**" against a "low-quality deterministic **redraw**", **not** "deterministic vs truly random"; this does not affect the five time-side criteria (all of them are **structural**). ② "The eye cannot tell" was operationalized only through **block-density variance** (8.7% apart); **no** information-theoretic model of the visual system was built (the real system involves sparse sampling, contrast response and motion detection, which are richer than a block average). ③ This round **does not** adjudicate "whether tick is the more fundamental choice" — that is the postulate of §8.6.7, not a result here.
> ⟹ **Consistent with round 43**: "**no information loss**" refers to the **state**, while "'**entropy growth**' refers to **our ignorance of the state**" — the nine criteria are the **visual image** of that sentence: the state side (k, reversibility, line-level rearrangement) is **strictly conserved**, whereas the ensemble side (coarse-grained readout) takes **62 steps** to homogenize.
> ⟹ Addendum: the simulator has a new panel ④ "snow-screen comparison ★" (tick left / white noise right, identical density, with an "ordered initial state" button); the panel-level test `_sim_snow_test.js` gives **6 pass / 0 fail**.

**【Honest boundary (8.6.7)】**

| Item | Nature |
|---|---|
| nearest-neighbour transposition mechanism, permutation-group properties | 【Derivation + numerics】 |
| the three exclusion criteria for parallel normalization | 【Derivation + numerics】, but rely on reading A1's ν as a per-element rate |
| bipartite perfect matching + random axis/phase | 【implementation choice】: "each pair independently decides whether to swap" (effective mobility 1/2) is also viable — scaling law unchanged, diffusion coefficient changed; we take the A1-commensurate one |
| ⟨r²⟩ = n l_P² | numerics (2D/3D cubic lattice + periodic boundaries, L = 10); the framework does not fix the lattice geometry |
| "effective degrees of freedom < number of sites" | observation (fixed-axis seed spread 375–630, far beyond the independent-sample 3σ = 27); no quantitative correlation length yet |
| adjacency (why nearest-neighbour rather than a 3-cycle) | **not argued**, inherited from the "hand-to-hand" picture |

#### 8.6.8 Substrate lattice geometry: 6 exterior-space directions ⟹ simple cubic (T-6 verdict, round 30)

> **【v2.9 verdict: the "diamond" of the earlier draft is withdrawn】** The earlier draft of this subsection (round 27) fixed the diamond structure from "coordination number = direction-cluster number k = 4". Round 30 traced this back and found that step **does not hold**: §4.10 Argument 4 explicitly separates two direction sets — "regular-tetrahedron 4 directions = **interior-space** component structure; **octahedral 6 directions = exterior-space axis structure (3 axes ±)**; entropy encoding uses interior-space components ⟹ direction-cluster number 4".
> ⟹ **The substrate lattice lives in exterior space ⟹ coordination number 6 ⟹ simple cubic**; the "direction-cluster k = 4" belongs to the **interior-space / entropy-encoding** level and does not fix the lattice coordination number.
> §4.9 Lemma 1 ("4 in-plane directions + 2 out-of-plane directions curled up") is exactly the statement "4 of the 6 neighbours are available for information transfer" — the two are consistent.

> **【Round-84 audit note (C-36): rank of the grounds for the T-6 verdict】** The claim of **"three independent grounds"** in item (i) above is **overstated** — none of the three is an independent mathematical theorem: ① §4.10 **Argument 4** is labelled 【**structural argument**】 (end of §4.10: "Theorems 1 and 3 rigorous; Theorem 2 established; **Argument 4 a structural argument**"); ② §4.9 **Lemma 1** is labelled 【**framework assumption**】 (round 29 corrected it from "a derivation of this framework" ⟹ **no mechanism**); ③ the "discriminator" language in (i) turns a **matching** into a **discrimination** — Lemma 1 is a statement about **information channels** (see C-34), and using it to fix the geometry requires the extra reading "4 of the 6 neighbours are usable", itself labelled 【framework assumption】 (honest-limits table).
>
> ⭐ **The genuinely hard grounds live in §4.8**: the number of encoding directions is forced to 4 by two **counting constraints** — **`k·q = 16` (no integer solution at k = 6: 16/6 = 2.667)** and **`2^k = 16 ⟹ k = 4` (k = 6 gives 64 states)** — and both derive from **B5's "raw readable 4 bits/plaquette" plus the BH temperature calibration** (§4.8, round 33: `w = 0` is *solved for* jointly by the BH entropy calibration and full occupancy, not imported as an assumption).
>
> ⟹ **The correct — and harder — reason why "the diamond fails"**: the 4 encoding connections of §4.8 are **the bonds from a site to its 4 in-plane neighbours (±x, ±y)** (§4.9, round 35), and those 4 directions are **coplanar**; whereas the 4 bonds of diamond point along the **regular-tetrahedron directions (non-coplanar)** ⟹ **no plane in diamond can contain all 4 of its bonds** ⟹ **coplanarity is what fails**.
>
> ⚠️ **This also restores the standing of the round-27 reading**: round 27 fixed the lattice bond directions from "direction cluster k = 4 (Tammes regular tetrahedron)", and that inference **is self-consistent under the reading "direction cluster = exterior-space bond directions"**; round 30 instead adopts the **layered reading** (the direction cluster belongs to the **information / interior** layer and does not fix the geometric coordination number). ⟹ This is **not "round 27 got it wrong" but a contest between two readings**, and the framework adopts the latter. **The conclusion (simple cubic) is unchanged, but the wording should be read as "the attribution on which that step rested is not adopted" rather than "that step fails".** The rank of "coordination number 6 ⟹ simple cubic" is 【**verdict (one of two readings)**】, not 【theorem】.

> **【Round-85 correction note (C-37): two over-corrections of the round-84 note are withdrawn — the audit missed the memo family】** ⚠️ The round-84 note above was produced by reading **the paper only**, **without the memos** ⟹ two of its conclusions were **too harsh** and are now corrected:
>
> **① The charge against item (i) is withdrawn.** Round 84 wrote "③ the 'discriminator' language in (i) turns a **matching** into a **discrimination**" — **that charge does not hold**. Item (i) is an **exact enumeration** (round-31 RC-5; the memo reads: "exact enumeration — the extremum must occur in a plane spanned by some pair of directions, so it suffices to enumerate direction **pairs**, **no sampling needed**"): the **maximum number of coplanar directions** is **4** (normal = the out-of-plane axis) for simple cubic / octahedral **✅ passes**, and **2** for diamond / regular tetrahedron **❌ fails** (the 4 tetrahedral directions are **non-coplanar**, any plane holds at most 2). ⟹ This is a **mathematical argument** (necessary condition plus exhaustive enumeration) and **is formally valid**. The only correct qualification concerns its **premise**: it relies on Lemma 1's "4 + 2" requirement, and Lemma 1 is a 【framework assumption】 ⟹ **it is not a ground independent of Lemma 1** (as the honest-limits table already states).
>
> **② The "two equal readings" characterisation and the "restoration of the round-27 reading" are withdrawn.** Round 84 wrote "this is not 'round 27 got it wrong' but **a contest between two readings**" and on that basis **restored** the standing of reading I — **an over-correction**. What the memo family records is:

**(a) Hard constraints (retained)**: all nearest-neighbour transpositions within one tick form a **perfect matching** (every vertex covered exactly once). Requiring every direction to occur (isotropy) means the edges must be covered by d perfect matchings ⟹ the graph must be **d-regular and bipartite**; by **König's edge-colouring theorem** its edges are exactly d-colourable, each colour being a perfect matching.

**(b) Geometry after the verdict**: exterior-space axis structure = 3 axes ± ⟹ **simple-cubic lattice**, coordination number **6**, bipartite (checkerboard colouring). Numerics (L = 8): n = 512, A = B = 256, m = 3n = 1536.

**(c) König edge-colouring realised**: **3 axes × 2 phases = 6 perfect matchings** (each vertex covered exactly once; 6 × n/2 = m ✓). Each tick draws an (axis, phase) at random and swaps along that axis in odd–even pairs.

**(d) Number density and collision rate (original values restored)**: simple-cubic lattice spacing = l_P ⟹ n = **1/l_P³**, collision rate nσv = **1/t_P** (σ = l_P²; residual 1.2×10⁻⁷ from l_P and t_P each being rounded to 7 digits), mean free path = **l_P**.
⟹ The diamond-based 0.6495/l_P³, 0.6495/t_P and 1.5396 l_P of the earlier draft are **withdrawn together**; and the ambiguity "bond length = l_P ⟹ density depends on the embedding (0.6495 vs 5.196, a factor 8 apart)" **does not arise for simple cubic**.

**(e) Numerical verification** (L = 8, N = 512, 1500 ticks, 5 seeds): ⟨x²⟩ = 498.95, ⟨y²⟩ = 493.42, ⟨z²⟩ = 506.70 (expected n/3 = 500 each), ⟨r²⟩ = 1499.07 (expected n = 1500), zero vacuum events, zero non-permutation ticks.

**(f) ⚠️ The matching must be locally randomised**: using only "pick one direction globally per tick and swap along all its edges" makes the entire substrate **rigidly translate**, because all states share one direction sequence. Fix: **draw an odd–even phase independently per line**. ⟹ **"Global synchrony = rigid translation, not a random walk"**, reproduced independently on both simple cubic and diamond.

**(g) A 6-regular bipartite graph is non-planar too**: a bipartite planar graph requires `m ≤ 2n − 4`, while 6-regularity gives `m = 3n` ⟹ contradiction (n = 512, m = 1536, bound 1020). ⟹ **The four-colour theorem (which speaks only of planar graphs) does not apply to the substrate**; regarded as a "map", the chromatic number is set by Heawood from the **genus**, independently of the coordination number (see the LST/Borsuk analysis in §4.10).

**(g′) 【withdrawn in round 83】** ⚠️ In round 82 a paragraph was added here claiming that a **4-regular** bipartite graph (the **diamond** lattice) is non-planar. **Its premise was wrong and it is withdrawn**: item (b) of this subsection already fixes the **simple-cubic lattice with coordination number 6**, and **6-regular = simple cubic = the substrate's current geometry** ⟹ **(g) already covers it**. The "4-regular (coordination 4)" case belongs to the **round-27 earlier draft's "diamond"**, which the **round-30 T-6 verdict has already withdrawn** (see the verdict block at the head of this subsection, and item (i): "**the diamond structure is incompatible with §4.9 Lemma 1**"). ⟹ **No 4-regular substrate geometry exists in the framework**; that case **has no object** — it is not "a missing computation" but "a computation that should not be made". The inequality itself is correct (a 4-regular bipartite graph is indeed non-planar: `m = 2n` versus the bound `2n−4`, a contradiction for every n), but **the framework cannot use it**. ⭐ Lesson: **retrieving a word ≠ that word being current** (here 4 of the 5 occurrences of "diamond" in the base document carry withdrawal markers). **Registered as C-33.**

- **(i) Third independent support (round 31): Lemma 1's "4 in-plane + 2 out-of-plane" is a
  discriminator for the lattice geometry.** Exact enumeration (the extremum is attained on a

### 10.1b Numerical reliability assessment (v2.3, response to referee M3)

**Criterion calibration (κ_c)**: fine-grid (52 points) 4-size torus data analysis — internal criteria (m=0.5 / χ peak / Δ minimum) give 0.11–0.17 by 1/L extrapolation, **systematically low by 47–67%** relative to the literature exact value h_c≈0.3285 (iPEPS) (the toy-model scale gap is quantitatively confirmed); **decisive diagnosis: m(h_c) extrapolates to 0.93 ≠ 0 with L ⟹ ⟨X⟩ (single-site magnetization) is not the transition order parameter** (mechanism-level confirmation of the v1.23 "m=0.5 is a half-polarization point" criterion correction); χ_max ∝ L^{1.12} (2D-Ising-like γ/ν=1.75) supports the existence of the transition, broadened by small lattices. **Calibration conclusion**: the true transition point is taken as the literature value **κ_c≈0.25–0.32** (framework symmetric convention equivalent to the literature, v1.23), with the criterion basis upgraded to "literature calibration + string order parameter ⟨W⟩ + Binder crossing + large-lattice MPS/DMRG".

**λ_c finite-size boundary**: 4-size χ_max ∝ L^{−0.04} (not growing) ⟹ the χ peak is a broadened pseudo-peak; λ_χ shows no drift (all 0.140) ⟹ v1.36's [0.140, 0.140] is a **rod/plate (thinnest direction = 1) pseudo-critical value**.

**【v2.9 supplement】true 3D-box DMRG (A1)**: 2×2×2 (36 faces) and 3×2×2 (52 faces), hand-written 1-site MPS/DMRG (χ=8) — χ peaks λ_χ(2×2×2)=0.230, λ_χ(3×2×2)≈0.19–0.21.

**【round 101 (item 甲-2) correction: `λ_c(∞)` is still NOT localized, and A1's [0.21, 0.23] was over-interpretation】** (scripts `_lc3d_box_fast.py` / `_lc3d_run_101.py` / `_lc3d_analyze.py` / `_lc3d_chitest.py`; the solver was validated against exact diagonalization on 2×1×1 to 1e-15 and **reproduces A1's 2×2×2 data point-by-point to 5e-5**) — **four corrections**:
① **with 4 sizes (2×2×2 / 3×2×2 / 3×3×2 / 3×3×3, including a true L=3) the peak does not converge**: coarse grid 0.2425→0.2459→0.2826→**0.3075**; A1's "no drift" came from **two sizes whose shortest edge is both 2** (not independent scaling points);
② **the criterion's resolution is insufficient, and extrapolation without error bars is not possible**: the χ-peak **grid dependence grows with size** (0.008→0.018→**0.025**) and is **of the same order** as the size drift (~0.03); the fine-grid sequence 0.2348→0.2281→0.2572 is **non-monotonic**; 1/L̄ extrapolation gives **0.436** (**outside the entire data range** — self-evidently absurd);
③ **`χ = −d⟨A_c⟩/dλ` is not a reliable transition criterion in this model** (same family as the 2D diagnosis already in this section: "m(h_c) extrapolates to 0.93 ≠ 0 ⟹ ⟨X⟩ is not the order parameter"); and the **Binder cumulant is identically useless** (`A_c² = I` ⟹ ≡ 2/3); the only relatively strong criterion this round is the **cross-size crossing point of ⟨A_c⟩**, clustered at **0.32–0.34**;
④ **the χ=8 truncation error is measured at 1–3%** (on 2×2×2, χ=8→16 shifts ⟨A_c⟩ by +0.0205, ΔE by 1.7%) — ⚠️ and because 1-site DMRG's discarded weight is **identically 0** (no bond growth) ⟹ **it cannot serve as a quality metric**, so the L=3 error **cannot be quantified**.
⟹ **Verdict: `λ_c(∞)` is not solvable by this method** (registered as "methodologically insufficient — not localized", no number given). Localizing it requires a **different order parameter** ('t Hooft/Wilson-loop perimeter–area-law crossing) ＋ a **different algorithm** (PEPS / 3D DMRG / QMC — this model has no sign problem) ＋ an **exact duality map**. See the memo 《甲2结项_λc∞未定位_判据分辨率不足的量化》.

**【Round 102 (item 甲-3) toric-code transition: the numerics cannot discriminate `1/3` from `0.3285`, and 「h_c = K/J」 has no mechanism】** (scripts `_toric102.py` / `_toric102_run.py` / `_toric102_analyze.py`; **sector-vs-full-space cross-check** ΔE₀~1e-14, Δm~1e-13; m(h=0.35)=**0.376308** on 3×3 matches the earlier record) — **four points**:

> **【v2.9 review (round 82: plugging "Poincaré conjecture + Ricci flow" into the framework? — four mismatches; registered as NOT COVERED)】** The proposal (user): *"a high-dimensional manifold is homeomorphic to a sphere; **Ricci flow is the annihilation of positive/negative elements**, turning concave parts convex and convex parts concave, ending as a sphere = the **elementary particle**; and **quantum entanglement is the connection point that Ricci flow cannot eliminate in high dimensions**."* Reviewed item by item (script `_ricci_82.py`, **RC-1 … RC-9, 9 checks, 0 failures**):
> · ⚠️ **① Dimension mismatch**: **Ricci flow proved only `n = 3`** (Perelman 2002–03); `n ≥ 5` is **h-cobordism** (**Smale 1961**, **unrelated to Ricci flow**, 40 years earlier); the `n = 4` topological case is Freedman (1982, disk embedding); the **`n = 4` smooth case remains open**. ⟹ **the framework's substrate is 4-dimensional (3 space + 1 time)**, exactly the dimension where "**Ricci flow cannot decide and the smooth case is still open**".
> · ⚠️ **② Mechanism mismatch**: Ricci flow does **not** turn "concave into convex and convex into concave"; it is "**higher curvature contracts faster**" (`∂g_ij/∂t = −2R_ij`) — convexity differences are **amplified**, not swapped. Numerically (discrete curve-shortening flow): a **convex** ellipse's shape deviation goes **0.3242 → 0.0446** (**rounds up** ✓ **Gage–Hamilton 1986**); a **dumbbell**'s `r_min` goes **0.5750 → 0.0377** (**neck pinch**, the thin neck breaks first). And a unit `S³` under the unnormalized flow gives `g(t) = (1−4t)g₀` ⟹ it **collapses to a point** (`t_c = 1/4`), rather than "becoming a sphere" (it already is one).
> · ⚠️ **③ Object mismatch**: the framework's "particle" is a **direction cluster with `k = 4`** (§4.10 Theorems 1+2 = the LST/Borsuk chain), and the **substrate network is intrinsically non-planar** (see §8.6.8 **(g)**: a **6-regular = simple-cubic** bipartite graph is necessarily non-planar — ⚠️ round 82 cited (g′) "4-regular/diamond", **withdrawn in round 83**; see the (g′) item of §8.6.8) ⟹ it is a **discrete graph**, **not a smooth manifold**; "particle + sphere" co-occur in the body **0 times**.
> · ⚠️ **④ Layer mismatch**: a connection point that **cannot be eliminated** is the **opposite** of Ricci flow's essence — its core operation is **surgery** (**cut the neck off and glue in spheres**, the key step in Perelman's proof) ⟹ "**it can be cut**" is the whole point; whereas the framework's existing "connection point" is the **quantum line / cross-branch ER channel**, which round 55 already ruled to **carry information only, never energy** (ANEC + the quantum inequality locking the budget to `ΔE·Δt ≲ ℏ`). Also, **entanglement belongs to the quantum-state layer**, and the framework **has no such layer** (round 76: non-commutativization is **external**).
> · ⭐ **Fair recognition (positive)**: the proposal **is not idle speculation** — it corresponds to two **real theorems**: **Gage–Hamilton (1986, convex curves ⟹ round)** and **Perelman (2003, simply connected 3-manifolds ⟹ S³)**. ⚠️ But the four mismatches remain ⟹ **four-way classification = postdiction (restating mathematics) + failure (the mapping does not hold)**, with **no genuine prediction**; and **decidability = 0** (mass spectrum / spin / coupling constants / entanglement-entropy coefficient / singularity window — each is **same-value, already ruled out, or no route**).
> · 📌 **Precedent warning**: line 1254 already records that "**`S³` is parallelizable ⟹ the hairy-ball theorem does not apply (corrected in an earlier version)**" — the framework **once** took "a topological theorem about spheres" at face value and **had to correct it on dimensional grounds**; this proposal is **exactly the same class of risk**.
> ⟹ **Registration: the framework does NOT cover the chain "Ricci flow → elementary particle"** (same type as **T-10** / **S-12**). **N-37**.

### 10.3 Relation to mainstream physics

- **Does not overthrow GR/QM**: the framework positions itself as the "statistical-mechanical foundation" of both (GR is the geometric description, the framework is the statistical-fluid description; the non-commutative structure of QM gains an ontological motivation);
- **Relation to candidate paths**: structural correspondences with Sakharov induced gravity, Verlinde entropic force, 't Hooft cellular automata, causal-set theory, loop quantum gravity (area spectrum/ln 2), string theory (KK/holography) — the framework provides the "event-flow" language unifying these threads;
- **Honesty statement**: no 【To be verified】 item may be used as a basis for derivation; the mathematization of the framework has completed its main structure, but several micro-derivations still await rigorization.

## Appendix A Notation and conventions

- Metric signature (−,+,+,+); natural units ℏ=c=k_B=1 (unless stated);
- M_Pl = 1.22×10¹⁹ GeV; l_P = 1.62×10⁻³⁵ m; t_P = 5.4×10⁻⁴⁴ s;
- ε_A = energy-scale-dependent leakage probability; ε_B = statistical decay factor;
- 【Axiom】 = fundamental postulate; 【Assumption】 = physical assumption introduced by this framework; 【Derivation】 = framework-internal derivation; 【Citation】 = established result; 【To be verified】 = conjecture requiring verification.

## Appendix B Sources and AI-assistance statement

The physical ideas of this framework originate from the author's own research exploration; during conception and derivation, large language models were used as computational and drafting aids (2026). **All scientific claims, derivations, approximations, and numerical results were proposed and verified by the author**; the language models served only as derivation and writing assistants and do not constitute a scientific authority. The numerical scripts (attachments to the P0–P3 reports) reproduce all numerical results in this paper.
